Reuse first; the builder lists reuse vs replace before touching anything. The 5 currently certified patterns stay on the paper desk at tiny size with trust scores starting at ₹1.

2.13 The nine engines: 1 [redacted] Price Engine = the 35-ruler family, Door A, this judge; 2 Claude Price Engine = wider rulers + yardstick + absorbed Overnight/Insurance/Context; 3 YouTube, 4 Calendar, 8 Deliverables = stated patterns under this judge, paper at tiny size from admission; 5 Machine Factory = Door B + new rulers, nightly, RAM-gated; 6 Arbitrage and 7 Copycat Roof unchanged; 9 Rain Engine per 2.17.

2.14 Superseded from v1: three fixed cadence classes; the 70/20/10 sealed holdout as proof; trades counted as independent proof; futures as vehicles on ₹2L books; brute force as the trading family; any re-search slower than nightly.

2.15 What only data can answer: whether any pattern's trust score grows on data that arrives after it was stated; whether paper fills match real fills; whether the search beats the yardstick. Nothing here promises an edge; it refuses to be fooled.

2.16 One floor, separate rooms. Floor = the shared plumbing: data shelf, login and cameras, paper broker and costs, the judge, the checker, the engine bus, day-log, site, Telegram; it has its own daily PASS line, checklist section and clean-day count. Room = one engine: own folder, own tests runnable alone, own ledger and paper account, own checklist section, own daily PASS/FAIL line, own clean-day counter, own site page. The bus runs rooms in isolation: a room's crash = that room's FAIL, the next room runs. A floor failure is reported as "floor down — all rooms held" and counted against the floor, never as engine failures. Delivered, per room (OWNER-SAID): built → verified by an independent session → running its own N clean days on the desk with its record on its own page, independent of every other room; N is the checklist's clean-day rule.

2.17 Engine 9: Rain Engine (OWNER-SAID). A separate domain (weather → NCDEX RAINMUMBAI, ₹50 per mm, June–September, not on Kite) and a sovereign codebase: its own repo (rain-predictor), its own floor (weather data, its own truth chain, cloud collection on GitHub Actions), its own referee for forecast skill; its code, vault and collectors never migrate. It shares the operating model, the build loop, Telegram ([RAIN], send-only), a read-only site room, and this judge's code as a library for its paper calls. Its clean-day counter = its honest-day clock; its paper account = the fictional-one-lot paper caller, unlocked only by that clock. No edge claim before a full monsoon of scores vs climatology and market-implied. 2026's season is ending; off-season = close it honestly, status read, fix, wire the judge and the site room; the first full delivered season = June 2027. Rain blocks are labelled → RAIN and start with the rain folder path.

2.18 Yardstick and reference (Door-A idea #1 of the Claude Price Engine, stated before any search). Yardstick: buy the top slice of the 210 by 6-month return, hold one month, cash, tiny size, same judge — a plain, explained rule with an economic reason (momentum). The 6-month lookback and one-month hold are CONVENTIONS (academic standard); 3- and 12-month variants run as near-copies and are merged. Reference: hold the 210 equal-weight — the zero-skill line every long pattern must beat.

2.19 Formulas the code implements (LEAD-PROPOSED 28 Sep, new lead chat). The plain words above govern; if a formula clashes with them the builder reports the clash instead of choosing.
- Trust score T per Door-A pattern: T_0 = 1. Each trading day t after close: r_t = the day's net rupee result of the pattern's combined paper bet ÷ the rupees deployed that day (costs and worse-than-mid fills already inside; all that day's fills are one bet; no trade → r_t = 0). T_{t+1} = T_t × (1 + r_t). For cash stocks r_t > −1, so T never reaches zero and a benched pattern can climb back.
- Safe to check nightly: for a pattern with no edge after costs, T cannot drift up on average, so the chance it EVER crosses a level 1/α is at most α, however often it is checked.
- The bar: B = N_A ÷ α, where N_A = the number of ADMITTED Door-A patterns competing (past the admission test, near-copies merged; the generator's whole family does NOT count) and α = the shared luck allowance, a declared convention with reason and sensitivity stated in this file, never a bare typed number. Expected false promotions across all patterns ≤ α.
- Print, don't hide: time-to-promotion is set by the pattern's real daily edge and by B. Chunk 2's report prints, for fake streams of stated daily edge, the median days to cross B at the chosen N_A and α. "0 promoted" for a long time is the expected reading.
- Floor F (bench below it) and recent window W (the days holding the pattern's last k trades) are declared conventions with reasons; live needs T ≥ B and the product of (1 + r_t) over the last W days ≥ 1.
- Size from trust: m = clip((ln T − ln F) ÷ (ln B − ln F), 0, 1) × the engine's maximum fraction per position; caps per stock, family and day apply after m.

====
PRICE ENGINE V2 — the whole design in plain words (28 Sep 2026)
Governs: [redacted] Price Engine and Claude Price Engine, and the judge used by every other idea (YouTube, Calendar, Deliverables, Rain).

2.1 Purpose. The machine looks for trading patterns in NSE stocks, proves or disproves each with real money maths, and trades only proven ones on paper, by itself, every day. Most days will say "0 promoted". That is the machine working.

2.2 The grid (data). Rows = one stock at one minute, 2020 to today (~210 F&O stocks, plus NIFTY/BANKNIFTY futures bars); daily rows are stacked from minutes. Columns = rulers. Answer columns on the right = what happened after each row over the next hour / 1 day / 3 days / 5 days, after real charges and worse-than-mid fills. Every cell is stamped with when it became knowable. The stock list is kept as it was on each date. Prices are adjusted for corporate actions with the factors kept separately. A bad-data gate freezes any stock with missing minutes or bad ticks that day. The free NSE daily files (FII/DII, delivery %, participant OI, corporate actions, results dates) are extra columns.

2.3 Rulers. One number per stock per bar. [redacted]'s 35 stay exactly as they are; the Claude Price Engine adds wider families (price/trend, volume, volatility, flows, market state, calendar; options and order-book only once recorded). Reactive rulers describe now (RSI is 28); predictive rulers are forecasts made now (tomorrow's range ≈ 2%). A predictive ruler enters conditions only after its forecasts beat "tomorrow is like today" on data it never saw. New rulers come from the Machine Factory; rulers are never deleted; sanity checks use mathematical ranges only.

2.4 A pattern = condition + direction + hold + exit.
- Buckets: each ruler's readings are cut into a few equal-count slices from the stock's own history (or across stocks on the day). Any slice can be the bucket — top, bottom or middle — and two neighbouring slices may be merged. Edges are always computed; the number of slices is a declared convention (reason + sensitivity stated), kept small.
- Condition: one or two rulers each in a bucket, true in the same row. Any mix of reactive and predictive rulers.
- Fires: the rows where the condition is true. Their answer columns are the pattern's record. Both directions are tested.
- Hold: the pattern keeps the answer column where net profit is best above the cost floor; nothing under an hour survives costs. Exits (fixed stop, time, trailing) are separate patterns from the same condition. Patterns never become rulers.
- Conditions are checked on finished bars: daily patterns at the close, intraday patterns each finished bar.

2.5 Two doors. Door A (trading): patterns stated before searching — the 35 rulers × buckets × holds (a few thousand), the Claude rulers likewise, the YouTube rules, the 323 Facts, the deliverables, the yardstick of 2.18. Door B (research): the brute-force millions and new rulers, scored every night, never deleted, never promoted directly; a Door-B pattern that keeps looking good is re-stated as a Door-A pattern and starts from zero. Reason: the luck bar rises with the number of patterns competing for promotion; thousands can be judged honestly on six years, millions cannot.

2.6 Two bars. Admission (past data): the pattern's history split by years, fitted on some, tested on years it never saw, costs inside, near-copies merged — passing is permission to enter the paper desk at tiny size, a shortlist, not proof. Trust score (future data): each Door-A pattern starts with ₹1 of pretend money; once per day after close it moves by the day's net result of the pattern's paper trades (fees and slippage taken out); twenty stocks bought the same afternoon are one bet, one move. A worthless pattern's pretend money cannot keep growing; when it crosses the bar the pattern is promoted. The bar is set from how many Door-A patterns compete (one shared luck allowance), so a worthless pattern almost never crosses it in its life however often you check — nightly checking is safe. Near-copies are merged and judged as one. Instances, not variety: a pattern rises or falls only on its own days of proof.

2.7 The ladder. bench → found → testing → paper (tiny) → live (full) → back down when the score falls under its floor. Nothing is ever deleted.

2.8 Alpha decay. Live needs lifetime AND recent-window pretend money both positive (window derived from the pattern's own trade frequency); recent negative → demoted. Losing days shrink the score; under the floor → bench. Recency may shrink or suspend, never promote. Benched patterns keep being scored and climb back on their own if they recover. If promoted patterns decay systematically right after promotion, the judge itself is flagged wrong and promotions stop until fixed.

2.9 One day. Morning: the owner's one login; yesterday's bars + NSE files pulled and checked; bad stocks frozen. Market: only paper and live patterns act, on finished bars at the next bar's open; P&L live; the laptop runs only the desk. Close: booked. Night: today's row added to every pattern ever created; every trust score updated; every status re-decided; new patterns admitted (Door A a few per night, limited by measured speed; Door B within the RAM floor); nothing deleted; one status line; the checker runs. Owner's rules honoured: daily search, daily scoring, after-hours search, live money updates.

2.10 Money. ₹2,00,000 paper per engine, set once. Cash stocks only; shorts intraday only; index and stock futures are data (one Nifty lot ≈ ₹16 lakh); Overnight and the index tests are diaries in points and ₹ per lot until a book big enough for one lot exists (a later money decision); options later, spreads with a fixed maximum loss only. Size from the trust score: tiny when new, bigger as trust grows; caps per stock, per family, per day; daily-loss breaker; KILL/RESUME. Every paper trade pays real Zerodha charges and worse-than-mid fills; a stop crossed by a gap fills at the real open; a halted stock cannot be exited until it trades. Real money stays closed.

2.11 The checker. A separate program, not a Claude session, re-adds the money from raw fills every night and must match to the rupee. "Live" means the loop reproduces its own logs, not that a pattern makes money. Paper fills are estimates until months of depth recording exist; fills are re-calibrated monthly.

2.12 Build order (each chunk ends with a measured report; no ETA before chunk 3): 0 data check + as-of stamps + the as-it-was stock list → 1 cost model + paper broker matched to the rupee (gaps, halts, expiry) → 2 the judge on fake data → 3 Door-A generator + near-copy merging + admission test + speeds measured (first honest ETA) → 4 nightly loop + checker + status line wired into the running desk, verify-gated by an independent session → 5 long paper run + monthly fill calibration + yardstick comparison. Reuse first; the builder lists reuse vs replace before touching anything. The 5 currently certified patterns stay on the paper desk at tiny size with trust scores starting at ₹1.

2.13 The nine engines: 1 [redacted] Price Engine = the 35-ruler family, Door A, this judge; 2 Claude Price Engine = wider rulers + yardstick + absorbed Overnight/Insurance/Context; 3 YouTube, 4 Calendar, 8 Deliverables = stated patterns under this judge, paper at tiny size from admission; 5 Machine Factory = Door B + new rulers, nightly, RAM-gated; 6 Arbitrage and 7 Copycat Roof unchanged; 9 Rain Engine per 2.17.

2.14 Superseded from v1: three fixed cadence classes; the 70/20/10 sealed holdout as proof; trades counted as independent proof; futures as vehicles on ₹2L books; brute force as the trading family; any re-search slower than nightly.

2.15 What only data can answer: whether any pattern's trust score grows on data that arrives after it was stated; whether paper fills match real fills; whether the search beats the yardstick. Nothing here promises an edge; it refuses to be fooled.

2.16 One floor, separate rooms. Floor = the shared plumbing: data shelf, login and cameras, paper broker and costs, the judge, the checker, the engine bus, day-log, site, Telegram; it has its own daily PASS line, checklist section and clean-day count. Room = one engine: own folder, own tests runnable alone, own ledger and paper account, own checklist section, own daily PASS/FAIL line, own clean-day counter, own site page. The bus runs rooms in isolation: a room's crash = that room's FAIL, the next room runs. A floor failure is reported as "floor down — all rooms held" and counted against the floor, never as engine failures. Delivered, per room (OWNER-SAID): built → verified by an independent session → running its own N clean days on the desk with its record on its own page, independent of every other room; N is the checklist's clean-day rule.

2.17 Engine 9: Rain Engine (OWNER-SAID). A separate domain (weather → NCDEX RAINMUMBAI, ₹50 per mm, June–September, not on Kite) and a sovereign codebase: its own repo (rain-predictor), its own floor (weather data, its own truth chain, cloud collection on GitHub Actions), its own referee for forecast skill; its code, vault and collectors never migrate. It shares the operating model, the build loop, Telegram ([RAIN], send-only), a read-only site room, and this judge's code as a library for its paper calls. Its clean-day counter = its honest-day clock; its paper account = the fictional-one-lot paper caller, unlocked only by that clock. No edge claim before a full monsoon of scores vs climatology and market-implied. 2026's season is ending; off-season = close it honestly, status read, fix, wire the judge and the site room; the first full delivered season = June 2027. Rain blocks are labelled → RAIN and start with the rain folder path.

2.18 Yardstick and reference (Door-A idea #1 of the Claude Price Engine, stated before any search). Yardstick: buy the top slice of the 210 by 6-month return, hold one month, cash, tiny size, same judge — a plain, explained rule with an economic reason (momentum). The 6-month lookback and one-month hold are CONVENTIONS (academic standard); 3- and 12-month variants run as near-copies and are merged. Reference: hold the 210 equal-weight — the zero-skill line every long pattern must beat.

2.19 Formulas the code implements (LEAD-PROPOSED 28 Sep, new lead chat). The plain words above govern; if a formula clashes with them the builder reports the clash instead of choosing.
- Trust score T per Door-A pattern: T_0 = 1. Each trading day t after close: r_t = the day's net rupee result of the pattern's combined paper bet ÷ the rupees deployed that day (costs and worse-than-mid fills already inside; all that day's fills are one bet; no trade → r_t = 0). T_{t+1} = T_t × (1 + r_t). For cash stocks r_t > −1, so T never reaches zero and a benched pattern can climb back.
- Safe to check nightly: for a pattern with no edge after costs, T cannot drift up on average, so the chance it EVER crosses a level 1/α is at most α, however often it is checked.
- The bar: B = N_A ÷ α, where N_A = the number of ADMITTED Door-A patterns competing (past the admission test, near-copies merged; the generator's whole family does NOT count) and α = the shared luck allowance, a declared convention with reason and sensitivity stated in this file, never a bare typed number. Expected false promotions across all patterns ≤ α.
- Print, don't hide: time-to-promotion is set by the pattern's real daily edge and by B. Chunk 2's report prints, for fake streams of stated daily edge, the median days to cross B at the chosen N_A and α. "0 promoted" for a long time is the expected reading.

- Size from trust: m = clip((ln T − ln F) ÷ (ln B − ln F), 0, 1) × the engine's maximum fraction per position; caps per stock, family and day apply after m.

tool_result: 7:Fee model (conservative, cited): Zerodha intraday-equity + NSE statutory round-trip on turnover, plus a
12:PAPER_R_RUPEES = 1000.0          # 1R = the rupee risk per call (R-E1-NOTIONAL: 0.5% of capital)
13:NOTIONAL_CAPITAL = 200000.0      # the stated paper capital returns/drawdowns are measured on (R-E1-NOTIONAL)
14:MAX_OPEN = 20                    # at most 20 concurrent paper positions, one per stock
15:CUSHION_M = 0.05                 # win-rate cushion added on top of breakeven
17:COST_STATUTORY_RT = 0.0010       # ~10 bps round-trip: brokerage(0.03%/leg cap Rs20)+STT(0.025% sell)+
18:                                 # NSE txn(0.00297%/leg)+SEBI+stamp(0.003% buy)+18% GST on charges
19:HALF_SPREAD = 0.0005             # 5 bps per side (conservative for liquid F&O names)
20:COST_RT = COST_STATUTORY_RT + 2 * HALF_SPREAD          # ~20 bps round trip (R-E1-FEE-PAPER)
22:MIN_ATR_FRAC = 1e-4              # a bar whose ATR < this fraction of price is flat/illiquid -> excluded
23:CLIP_R = 20.0                    # a >20-ATR move in a few bars is a data artifact -> clipped
26:#     for options/futures is WRONG — options costs are on the PREMIUM with FLAT Rs 20/order brokerage, and
27:#     futures STT is on the notional. These are Zerodha's published F&O charges (cited, not tuned). ---
28:FO_BROKERAGE_PER_ORDER = 20.0    # flat Rs 20/executed order, or 0.03% of turnover, whichever is LOWER
29:FO_BROKERAGE_PCT = 0.0003
30:STT_FUT_SELL = 0.0002            # 0.02% on the SELL side notional (futures)
31:STT_OPT_SELL = 0.0010            # 0.10% on the SELL side premium (options)
32:TXN_FUT = 0.0000173              # NSE 0.00173% of futures turnover
33:TXN_OPT = 0.0003503              # NSE 0.03503% of option premium turnover
34:SEBI_RATE = 0.000001             # Rs 10 per crore
35:GST_RATE = 0.18                  # 18% on (brokerage + transaction + SEBI)
36:STAMP_FUT_BUY = 0.00002          # 0.002% on the BUY side notional (futures)
37:STAMP_OPT_BUY = 0.00003          # 0.003% on the BUY side premium (options)
40:def _leg_cost(turnover, kind, side):
41:    """Zerodha statutory+brokerage for ONE leg. kind in {'fut','opt'}, side in {'buy','sell'}."""
42:    brokerage = min(FO_BROKERAGE_PER_ORDER, FO_BROKERAGE_PCT * turnover)
43:    stt = ((STT_FUT_SELL if kind == "fut" else STT_OPT_SELL) * turnover) if side == "sell" else 0.0
46:    gst = GST_RATE * (brokerage + txn + sebi)
47:    stamp = ((STAMP_FUT_BUY if kind == "fut" else STAMP_OPT_BUY) * turnover) if side == "buy" else 0.0

Per pattern\n1/B = \u03b1/N_A; summed over N_A patterns the expected false promotions <= \u03b1.\n\n\u03b1 is a DECLARED CONVENTION, never a bare typed number (\u00a72.19 / \u00a74.1):\n  ALPHA = 0.05 \u2014 the standard family-wise false-promotion allowance: across ALL admitted patterns the expected\n  number of worthless ones ever promoted is <= 0.05.
